> **Sprint S-BOOT-RACE — a false connection dialog over an app that had loaded, a column that was already there under another name, and a checkout that was half on another branch, 2026-09-29 (closing SHA: `3c38050` via PR #440, with `ed80d0c` via PR #441 and `7a40484` via PR #442).** **#441 `ed80d0c` — two races in `createMainWindow`, neither from the code installed that day.** Right after the #439 install on 2026-09-28 the box showed _"Không kết nối được tới http://127.0.0.1:3100 — ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED"_ twice while the app was up: the request log holds `GET /` 200 and the client calling `/api/health` before each dialog. (1) The app URL was loaded while the connecting screen was committed but still loading; Electron's `loadURL()` promise for the app records the connecting screen's abort (`-3`, empty description, on the `data:` URL) and rejects **after** the app page has loaded, and `main.js` read that as _server unreachable_ — one boot in eleven since 2026-09-24. (2) macOS `activate` fired during startup — a Dock click, or a second launch's reopen event while the embedded server comes up — and created a second main window; both async blocks loaded the module-level `mainWindow`, so the two loads aborted each other (`ERR_ABORTED` on the app URL). `connectThenLoad` now awaits the connecting screen before loading the app and drives only the window it is handed, and `activateAction` makes `activate` wait until startup has created the window. Both are pure, in `desktop/utils/mainWindowBoot.js`, so CI runs their tests; `connectThenLoad.test.js` reproduces the race in a real BrowserWindow (a slow image holds the connecting screen open while the server answers on commit), with a control pinning the Electron behaviour itself so a later Electron that stops misattributing the abort fails the control first. **Checked again before merging, on the tree it would land in**: #441 merged locally with `main` after #442 — desktop `utils` 27/27, and the Electron suites run locally only because CI has no display: `connect` 2/2, `sandbox` 5/5, `wizard` 4/4, `firstrun` 4/4. Dropping `await connecting` reddened the Electron test 1/2 and the unit tests 2/8, and was restored. The connecting screen's HTML carries no `src`, `href` or `url(`, so awaiting its load cannot hang on a resource. **Not in scope, from the PR**: the dialog still says `ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` for a genuine post-ready load failure, and its _Reset về Embedded + Restart_ calls `app.relaunch()` while the LaunchAgent's `KeepAlive` relaunches too — a double launch (MES-3-FIX-60). **#440 `3c38050` — the security review runs in every loop.** `cloudflare/security-audit-skill` (MIT) vendored into `.agents/skills/security-audit/` and pinned by `computedHash` in `skills-lock.json`; `.agents/rules/security-review.md` runs it in guidance mode on each iteration's diff after tests are green, confirmed Critical/High blocking; step 2b of the mandatory checklist points at the rule, and full-audit mode stays opt-in and writes outside the repo. The vendored directory is excluded from prettier and eslint — left in scope, prettier flagged nine of its files and eslint reported 76 errors in helper scripts nothing in the app, the server or CI loads, and those files must not be rewritten while a hash pins them. **#442 `7a40484` — CCL PN (80#) before Direct CU in Quote History, and the column was already there.** Henry asked for a new column; the table already had **IFS CODE**, hidden by default, whose cell rendered `state.ccl_pn` — the same value under a label that named something else (Lesson 21). Put to him, he chose renaming over a second column showing the same thing: key `ifs` → `ccl_pn`, label `CCL PN (80#)` in both languages (a trade term), moved before `direct_cu` and shown by default; a saved sort on `ifs` rewrites to `ccl_pn` (the `npi` → `owner` and `ver` → `option` pattern); a hidden-columns preference carrying `ifs` drops it as a stale key, so the column appears for everyone; `qh.ifs_code` and the `.qh-*-ifs` classes went as orphans of the change. 27 columns still, 23 visible by default instead of 22. Client **2349 → 2352 pass**; ESLint 0 errors on the touched files, the two warnings pre-existing; CI 10/10. Built from a worktree and INSTALLED per Lesson 37 (DMG sha256 `b72c78af…`, health 200 in 7 s): `qh.ccl_pn` 0 → 2 files and `qh.ifs_code` 2 → 0 in the installed bundle. **The first build lacked the two gitignored inputs Lesson 37's amendment names** — the NPI Parts snapshot and the autostart guide — found by diffing the fresh `client/dist` against the installed one before anything was swapped, copied in (the snapshot byte-identical to the installed copy) and rebuilt; the DMG's file list then matched the app's. The old server process was still alive after `bootout` when the `.app` was replaced; the port holder was checked afterwards and was only the new instance. Henry confirmed on screen (_"verify ok"_). **Installed together, 2026-09-29 09:43**: a DMG from `3c38050` (sha256 `e56f4c83…`) carrying #439–#442 — `connectThenLoad` 0 → 5 in the installed `app.asar` with `waitForServer` holding at 3 as the control, the `better_sqlite3.node` pair identical, health 200 in 7 s, 159 quotes / `integrity_check ok`, and no error line in the log since. That boot showed no dialog, which is an anecdote rather than evidence (Lesson 44): the race hit one boot in eleven, so the fix is established by the reproduction, not by one quiet start. **Operational, no PR — the checkout was half on another branch.** `.git/HEAD.lock`, 0 bytes, had sat in the shared checkout since 2026-09-28 10:55 with no git process holding it; S-SAVE-GUARD's build had already had to work around it. Removed with Henry's approval, it revealed what it had hidden: HEAD named `chore/add-security-audit-skill` (`d066ae6`) while the index and every file on disk were exactly `0c16803` — an interrupted switch to `main` that had written the tree and died before moving HEAD. `git diff --cached --quiet 0c16803 && git diff --quiet` confirmed nothing on disk was outside that commit before `git switch --discard-changes main` and a fast-forward; nothing was lost, and #440's branch was already on the remote. **A stale lock is not only an obstacle**: removing it can expose a half-finished operation, so compare the tree with HEAD before the next command writes anything.
>
> **Sprint S-TOOLTYPE-VISIBLE — the two tool-type findings S-SAVE-GUARD left open, fixed, 2026-09-28 (closing SHA: `0c16803` via PR #439).** **The stored type is always visible.** The tool-type cell is a `<select>` of today's list, so 114 rows on 54 quotes typed `Knife` (44), `Jig` (21), `Metal` (17), `Pinnacle Die` (15), `Rotary` (10), `Etching` (4) or `Carving` (3) read `--`; both grids now keep the stored type as an extra option (Lesson 55). **A tool with no life is flagged, and only that.** `toolLifeMissing` (`calcValidation.js`) fires when a row carries a tool (`tool_cost > 0` or a Layout `tool_cost_src`), its own tool life is 0, and neither today's list nor the quote's pricing snapshot gives the type a life — the case where `calcProcess` falls back to one shot. It drives a red cell with a tooltip in both grids and the validator error `proc-tool-nolife-N`. **Marking every old name was rejected on purpose**: most of those rows price correctly on their own tool life, and re-picking a type overwrites that life from the list, so a mark on all 114 would invite changes to correct quotes. On live data it flags **12 rows, four on each of Complex quotes 96, 97 and 113** (`RFQ-2026-C0001`, `Pinnacle Die`, a $1,000 tool) and no Standard row. **`Jig&Ficture` is a jig.** The engine's jig test normalised names to `jig` / `jigfixture`, and the list's own spelling normalises to `jigficture`, so a jig picked from the list was costed as a die multiplied by layout; it now accepts that spelling. An A/B of the old and new engine over the 106 live Standard quotes changes none, and a control row whose demand outlasts the jig moves 0.0625 → 0.1125 — which is what shows the A/B compared two different engines. **Verify, from the PR**: tests written first and watched failing on their own assertions; forcing the helper to `true` reddens exactly the cases it must not flag; client **2331 → 2349 pass**; ESLint 10's three extra error rules forced on the eight files after showing they catch a known dead store; build and perf budget pass with the shell at 531.7 of 537.1 kB and ComplexCalc at 96.7 of 97.7 kB, both 99.0% (MES-3-FIX-62). **Not in scope**: old names are not renamed and no tool life is changed; and the existing _Tool Type is required_ check reads the raw `tool_cost`, so a Layout-assigned tool with no type at all still escapes it. Installed on 2026-09-28 — the install after which S-BOOT-RACE's false dialog appeared — and again on 2026-09-29 inside `3c38050`.
>
> **Sprint S-SAVE-GUARD — the check moves to the moment of writing, and a workcenter the Rate Table no longer lists stops pricing in silence, 2026-09-28 (closing SHA: `6defcf6` via PR #436).** Both halves came out of re-saving `RFQ-2026-S0002` that morning. An edit enables Save and hides the #432 banner, so correcting one input carried an unrelated misread into the saved result — quote 101 persisted a GM of −238% — and the misread came from process rows naming workcenters the Rate Table no longer lists. Henry asked for two of three proposals (_"làm đề xuất 1 và 2 đi"_). **(1) The save checks what it is about to write.** `handleSave` builds the result Save will persist and compares it with the saved one through the banner's own `savedResultDrift` and its 50% test; when the move is implausible, the Update / Save-as-new dialog shows both subtotals and the share in a red block, and its default button becomes _Quay lại kiểm tra_, so Enter goes back — both save paths stay one click away. **This compares against what Save will write, which Lesson 53 forbids for the banner, and the difference is deliberate**: the banner asks whether the stored number still matches the screen and must not invite re-pricing every quote whose library prices moved; this check asks whether the write itself is sane, at the only moment it can still be stopped, and a library movement does not approach half a selling price. The dialog's strings moved to i18n, English kept word for word. **(2) A workcenter the Rate Table no longer lists is flagged.** Such a row reads a rate of 0 and no speed unit, so its overhead disappears and its speed is costed as pieces per operator-hour of hand labour — the 2026-08 manual-labour model. `unknownWorkcenter` (`calcValidation.js`) flags a name that is neither in the Rate Table nor frozen in the quote's own pricing snapshot, which still prices it, and stays quiet while the library is loading; the cell turns red with a tooltip naming it and the validator raises error `proc-wc-unknown-N`, Std and Cpx through the one helper (Lesson 48). **The names had been invisible, and that is the finding under the finding**: the grid's dropdown lists only current names, and a controlled `<select>` whose value matches no option shows its first — `--` — so the screen showed an empty workcenter while the data held `RDC12(Baytra)` (Lesson 55). The stored name is now kept as an extra option. **Measured on live data before shipping**: 26 rows on 11 quotes carry one of nine obsolete names — `FB`, `RDC12(Baytra)`, `Manual`, `Punch part`, `Punch tape`, `Flexo(Brotech2C)`, `Manual FQC`, `Manual Folding` (a case variant) and `Flexo(Gallus4C）` (a full-width parenthesis) — including all five quotes whose banner is red (27, 28, 103, 104, 154), and the 101 save would have been stopped at 254% of its price. **The first CI run was red, and the error was mine**: `let pending = null;` ahead of a `try`/`catch` that assigns in both branches is a dead store, which `no-useless-assignment` reports as an error. Local lint passed because `client/eslint.config.js` resolves `@eslint/js` from `client/node_modules` (9.39.4), whose recommended set lacks the rule, while CI installs only the root and gets 10.0.1 — **the rule #280 hit in September and put down to V8; it was the package version** (see the CI signal section). Forcing the three rules 10 adds over 9 reproduced CI's two errors at the same lines before the fix and read clean on all ten touched files after it; fixed in `c5c0bbe` the way #280 did it, and CI then read 0 errors and 413 warnings, the same as `main`. **Verify**: client **2313 → 2331 pass**; CI 10/10; the shell at 530.7 of 537.1 kB, 98.8% of the budget raised three days earlier (MES-3-FIX-62). Built in a separate worktree — the shared checkout was on another session's branch with its `HEAD.lock` held — from `c5c0bbe`, whose tree is identical to `6defcf6`'s (`a3f615a8`), and INSTALLED per Lesson 37 (DMG sha256 `c036559c…`, health 200 in 5 s, 157 quotes / 3063 materials / `integrity_check ok`, licence and role unchanged, 0 errors and 0 CSP violations; three updater timeouts, the box having no route to the update server). Markers: the five new strings 0 → 1–3 files; `Quote #$` 4 → 3, the file it left being the shared chunk that now carries the dialog; `pricing.drift.blocked_title` and `srd-banner--blocked` holding at 2 as controls; the installed `client/dist` byte-identical to the build. **What the clean worktree nearly dropped** (Lesson 37): two gitignored build inputs — the NPI Parts List data (`parts-snapshot.json`, 31.9 MB) and the autostart guide, which the `prebuild` hook does not generate. Found by diffing the build inputs before building, copied in, and confirmed afterwards by the DMG's file list matching the installed app's; without them the NPI Parts List tab would have opened empty. **And what the replaced bundle held**: five data files under `Contents/Resources/app/server/data` inside `/Applications/Ops Control.app` — an `ops.db` of 352 KB, a `users.json` with one account, `sessions.json`, `audit_log.json` and a `README_FIRST_LOGIN.txt` — created 9 s after the #432 install and never modified. Not the live data, which is untouched and holds five accounts. The new app's first boot created none, and no command of this session in that window loaded a server module from the bundle, so the cause is not established; what differed is that #432's install slept 2 s before replacing the app, while this one waited until both processes were gone and port 3100 was free. **Explained 2026-10-08 (Sprint S-CORE-OD)**: the files were in the DMG — the `../server` copy has no `data/**` exclusion, so a build from a checkout with a `server/data` ships it and a build from a clean worktree, as #436's was, does not; their birth time was the copy time. **On hardware, and the measurement went the other way.** Henry reported quote 103 (`RFQ-2026-S0058`) OK (_"đã verify 103 ok"_). The server log holds `PATCH /quotes/103 → 200` at 06:04:00Z: he had set the tool life of P3 and P4 from 0 to 200,000 — the only inputs that changed — and saved with the three unknown workcenters still in place. So 103 now persists the misread: subtotal 0.1599 → **1.1440** against a $0.1671 selling price, labour 0.0094 → 1.0030, overhead 0.0300 → 0, **GM 4.3% → −584.6%**; and because the saved result now equals the recompute, the banner no longer shows on it. The dialog cannot have been silent — the saved result is loaded with the quote and the move was 589% — so the write went through its _Vẫn cập nhật_; which button was pressed is not in the log. **Open, Henry's call**: re-pick the three workcenters and save (the mapping is his), or restore the row from `ops_20260927_190002.sqlite`, which holds it as last saved on 2026-04-01; and whether a move traced to an unknown workcenter should be refused rather than confirmed. **A slip of mine, the third of its kind in this file**: the first comparison of 103's two states defined its walker and never called it, and reported no change — one sentence from telling Henry he had changed nothing. Printing the number of fields compared — 381, 84 differing, 82 of them defaults an April quote gains on load — is what caught it (Lesson 43). **Reported and deliberately not fixed.** (a) The tool-type dropdown has the same exposure: 114 process rows on 54 quotes store a type the list no longer offers (`Knife` 44, `Jig` 21, `Metal` 17, `Pinnacle Die` 15, `Rotary` 10, `Etching` 4, `Carving` 3), and each reads `--`. Twelve of them, four on each of three Complex quotes — 96, 97 and 113, all `RFQ-2026-C0001` — carry `Pinnacle Die`, no tool life and a $1,000 tool, the combination that priced S0007 at $444/pc; their price effect is not measured, because a Complex recompute needs flags that live in the browser. (b) The engine recognises a jig by `jig` or `jigfixture` after lowercasing and stripping spaces and `&`, and the list's current name, `Jig&Ficture`, normalises to `jigficture` — so a jig picked from today's list is costed as a die, multiplied by layout. Latent: on the five live rows the number of tools agrees either way, demand being at most 38,400 against lives of 500,000 and more. **Both findings fixed 2026-09-28 by `0c16803` (PR #439, Sprint S-TOOLTYPE-VISIBLE); left as written because it is what was known then.** **Security review**, done by hand against the checklist another session is adding: no findings — the diff has no HTML sink, and the stored workcenter name reaches the page only as text, a `title` and an `<option>`, all escaped by React. **Resolved 2026-09-28 — 103 re-picked and saved.** Henry gave the names (_"Manual FQC --> FQC, RDC12(Baytra)--> RDC350-12"_), and with `Flexo(Gallus4C)` for the full-width variant all three exist in the Rate Table and are offered for their rows' process types (Print, Die_Cut, Inspection). He re-picked them and saved: `PATCH /quotes/103 → 200` at 07:41:05Z. Against the 06:04 state, 453 fields compared and 8 differ: the three names; `crew` 1 on each re-picked row, copied from the Rate Table; P6's scrap 0 → 0.1; and PO L/T `474~481 working days` → `7~14 working days` — the misread had inflated the production hours that rule reads, and the 06:04 save had persisted that too. 103 now stores a subtotal of **0.1984** (labour 0.0106, overhead 0.0329, tooling 0.0072), **GM −18.7%** at its $0.1671 price; no workcenter is flagged, the saved result equals the recompute, and the three stores agree (157 quotes, `integrity_check ok`, log clean). **The 10% was not typed**: picking a workcenter runs `resolveScrapOnWorkcenterChange` (`scrapDefaults.js`), which gives `FQC` — the only workcenter with a non-zero default, matched on the whole name — 10% whenever the row's scrap is still at a default value, a rule `Manual FQC` never triggered. My dry run wrote the names straight into the state and predicted 0.1806; recomputing the saved state with P6's scrap at 0 returns 0.1806 exactly, so the rule accounts for the whole difference (Lesson 54). Against the April result, material setup and run both rose 1.39× — today's library prices, frozen by the 06:04 save — ink setup rose with the #427 make-ready fix, and tooling with the 200,000-shot life bought in whole dies. **No xlsx of 103 went out while the misread was stored**: the audit log holds 15 `QUOTE_EXPORT` rows, the last on 2026-09-22, and none since; the request log carries no export lines at all, so it could not have answered the question, and a CSV from Cost Breakdown leaves no record. **Still open, Henry's call**: whether 10% FQC scrap applies to this job (without it, 0.1806 and GM −8.1%); the price, which is below cost either way; names for the six obsolete workcenters still unmapped; and whether a far-off save traced to an unknown workcenter should be refused rather than confirmed.

> **Sprint S-DRIFT-GUARD — "recalculate every quote" was the wrong question, and the banner that would have saved nonsense, 2026-09-28 (closing SHA: `af93214` via PR #432).** **The bulk recalculation, 2026-09-25 (operational, no PR).** Henry asked for the week's new calculation to be applied to every RFQ already made. A Save was never the tool for it: `buildQuoteData` re-freezes the pricing snapshot from today's library — re-pricing, not a formula update — and it moves `saved_at`, which the Cost Breakdown DATE column and Quote History's order both read, so a bulk save would have dated every quote to that afternoon. The update wrote **`result` only**, the display result computed against each quote's OWN snapshot, into all three stores the read path uses (Lesson 34): `quotes.result`, `raw_json.result` and `quote_history.json`. **A read-only dry run came first, and everything after rests on it.** It reproduces the app — CostLibContext's `lib` for site VN, `LOAD_QUOTE`'s `upgradeStdState`, #429's display calculation, the save path's rows and serialiser — and was **calibrated** before being believed: S0072 and S0073, saved after the #428 install, came back identical field for field. Then it said **103 of 106** Standard quotes would change, and the largest movers were nonsense: `RFQ-2026-S0007` 0.09 → **$444/pc** (a _Carving_ tool with no tool life falls back to 1), `RFQ-2026-S0002` → $165/pc, quote 27's labour +380% of its selling price (the 2026-08 manual-labour model reads RDC12(Baytra)'s speed per operator). **Most saved results carry drift from every engine change since their own save, not from this week's alone**: recomputing each quote with the engine from just before #427 reproduced exactly **one** of them (Lesson 54). So the scope was drawn from the data — a persisted snapshot (40 quotes have none, and for those "recalculate" means re-price at today's library), only ink and/or tooling moving, within ±15% of the selling price — **58 quotes**, which Henry chose over "September only" and "all 103". The largest writes: S0040 +14.0% of its selling price, S0003 +9.4%, S0050 +7.5%, S0069 +5.3%; S0059 and S0057, the two files Jet exported with a 0 tool cost, now carry the effective tool cost #426 persists. **The write itself was blocked by the permission classifier** — stopping the production server and bulk-writing live data is the owner's call — so the prepared block went to Henry and he ran it: stop via the LaunchAgent, `.backup` plus a copy of both stores (`*.before-bulk-recalc-20260925-163749`), one transaction, restart. **Verified against the backups, not against the plan**: exactly 58 rows changed, and only in `result` — not `saved_at`, not `updated_at`, not state; the three stores agree; the other 99 rows are byte-identical; `integrity_check ok`; and all 58 recompute to their stored value, so none shows the banner. The 45 out-of-scope quotes were left as chosen, and so were the 4 Complex ones, whose BOM-qty and MOQ-scaling flags live in each browser's `localStorage`, which no offline recalculation can know. **#432 `af93214` — the banner itself needed a guard.** #429 lit Save for ANY drift, and on those old quotes that is an invitation to write $444/pc into every file with one click. A drift now carries its size as a share of the selling price (of the saved subtotal when there is none), and more than half is `implausible`: the banner turns red and says to check tool life, speed and workcenter, and Save and Cmd+S stay off — through ONE shared `driftCanSave`, twins per Lesson 48 — until an edit. **The threshold was read off the data, not chosen**: of the 44 quotes still showing the banner, 40 move by under 31% of their price, 9 by 196% or more, and **none in between**; 0.5 sits in that gap, blocks all nine, and leaves S0017's +21.6% — checked by hand as correct in #427 — saveable. A drift with no subtotal to measure is never blocked, because it cannot be judged. **Verify**: client **2303 → 2313 pass**. The helper tests first failed at their IMPORT, which is a failure for the wrong reason, so the constant was exported alone to watch them fail on their own assertions (`share: undefined`); three sabotage checks each reddened exactly their own tests. ESLint 7 files / 0 messages, identical to `main`; shell 528.2 of 537.1 kB; CI 10/10. Built and INSTALLED per Lesson 37 (DMG sha256 `c54b8bf8…`, health 200 in 7 s, 157 quotes / 3063 materials / `integrity_check ok`, licence intact, 0 errors and 0 CSP violations): the red Vietnamese title 0 → 1 and `srd-banner--blocked` 0 → 2, with the amber title holding at 1 as the control; the normalised hashes moved on exactly `index`, `StandardCalc`, `ComplexCalc` and the shared chunk that carries the banner, and five controls held. **Stated rather than implied**: that S0007 shows the red banner with Save off is measured, not yet seen — run with the installed code over live data, exactly those nine come out red and the other 35 keep Save lit; seeing it on screen needs a login, which is Henry's to type. **Confirmed on hardware 2026-09-28 — and what Henry did is better evidence than looking would have been.** He opened `RFQ-2026-S0007` (_"đã kiểm S0007 ok"_), got the red banner with Save off, and corrected the input it pointed at: P2's tool type _Carving_ → `Knife/ Wood` and its tool life 0 → 20,000. Those are the only two values that changed against the 2026-09-25 backup — the other 80 changed paths are defaults an old quote gains on load, plus the snapshot the save froze. The edit re-enabled Save and he saved: `PATCH /quotes/116 → 200` at 01:38:33Z, stored subtotal 0.090095 → **0.105947** instead of $444.53, and on reopen it no longer drifts. That is the design working end to end: the guard stopped the broken number, named the input, and let the corrected quote through. **Two consequences, stated because the number hides them.** S0007 had no pricing snapshot, so that save froze one at today's library, and its new subtotal carries today's prices as well as the fix. And 20,000 shots against an `eauCap` of 19.2 million pieces makes the engine buy **1,067** dies — $0.0222/pc of tooling, 21% of the subtotal, GM 20.6%, under the floor. Whether 20,000 shots is that die's real life is Henry's to judge; Tool Life counts shots (S-TOOLLIFE-UNIT). **A probe note for whoever re-checks this**: `main.log` rotates at about 5 MB into `main.old.log` — the 2026-09-25 writes this entry cites now live there — so a log search that must reach back more than a couple of days has to read both files. The known-hit check of Lesson 43 is what showed it: the search for the 09-25 save came back empty from `main.log` alone. **Resolved 2026-09-28 — the question above had a third answer.** Henry identified the die: a carved **Carving/ NC Die** at $400. That is what the April quote had named `Carving`, a type the DDL no longer lists under that name (it has `Carving/ NC Die`, 40,000 shots), which is why the tool life fell back to 1 and the recalculation read $444/pc. The Knife/ Wood correction, although it cleared the guard, paired Knife's 20,000-shot life with the carved die's $400 price and was the most pessimistic of the three readings. P2 now reads `Carving/ NC Die`, 40,000 shots, $400 (`PATCH /quotes/116 → 200` at 03:38:00Z): 534 dies instead of 1,067, tooling $0.011125/pc, subtotal 0.094843, **GM 28.9%** — the figures the engine gave before the save — and no drift on reopen. A save twelve seconds after the quote was opened (03:35:45Z) had carried no tool-type change; whether one had been made on screen is not known, and this save did carry it. The MOQ 2 Target now stores 0 where it was blank, which does not touch the price. **The same name drift explains three more of the nine red quotes**: all three `RFQ-2026-S0002` rows carry tools typed `Knife` and `Jig` — the DDL now lists `Knife/ Wood` and `Jig&Ficture` — with a tool life of 0, and picking the type again from the dropdown refills the life from the DDL. The other five (quotes 27, 28, 103, 104 and 154) move through labour instead — the 2026-08 manual-labour model reading a speed per operator — so the remedy there is the speed or the workcenter, not the tool.
>
> **Sprint S-TOOL-ONLY-ROW — a process row with no machine may carry a die, and the size gate that was full, 2026-09-25 (closing SHA: `2230d50` via PR #431).** Henry ruled on the finding S-TOOL-COST-READERS reported, for reading A: _"Brotech với Gallus có trường hợp cả in và cắt all-in-line nhưng plate và cutter có tiền và tool life khác nhau nên cần điền thêm một dòng chỉ tính chi phí tool"_. A row carries one tool (`tool_cost_src` is a single source), so on an in-line print+cut press the plate rides on the press row and the die needs a second row with no workcenter — and `calcProcess` returned all zeros for such a row, tooling included, before it ever reached `effectiveToolCost`. **The tooling block moved into `processTooling()`, shared by both paths**, so a machine row and a tool-only row cannot compute a tool differently; a tool-only row is charged its tooling and nothing else. **Deleting the gate would have been the wrong fix**: a row that carries a speed but no machine speed takes the manual-labour path and is billed at the Manual rate — +$3.32/pc on S0069 — and one test exists to catch exactly that shortcut. `calcMatScrapFactor` already counts only rows WITH a workcenter, so a tool-only row's own scrap does not move the yield; the press row carries it. **The validator had to move with it**: a started row with no workcenter drew _"Workcenter is required"_ plus Speed and Efficiency complaints — S0069's die row carried `speed: 1`, very likely typed to silence the second — and obeying the first means picking the press again and billing its machine time twice. A row that carries a tool is exempt from those three; a row that does not is not, and the Tool Type check still applies. The Legend and F1 Help explain the two-row setup in both languages. **Measured, not assumed**: an A/B of the old and new engine over all 110 live quotes moved exactly one — S0069, tooling 0.000158 → 0.001622, subtotal 0.020638 → 0.022102, **GM 25.2% → 19.9%, under the 25% floor at its $0.0276 price**, reported to Henry as a pricing question. The 12 frozen fixtures are byte-identical: none carries such a row. **The size gate was full, and that is a finding of its own.** `Build artefacts` went red because main's shell measured **539,910 bytes against a 540,000 budget** — 90 bytes of headroom — and this fix adds 248. The July re-baseline gave the shell 458.8 kB plus about 15%, and two months of ordinary growth, the i18n waves among it, had used all of it. Raised to **550,000**, +1.9% over main, under the CI section's rule — legitimate multi-version growth, with MES-3-FIX-62 as the reduction ticket — and the budget comment that still named the Planning module (removed in #245) was corrected. **Verify**: client **2293 → 2303 pass**, server 1238 / 1 chronic skip; tests written first and watched failing with S0069's own persisted tooling (0.000157545); three sabotage checks, including deleting the gate outright; ESLint 0 errors with warnings identical to `main`; CI 10/10 once the budget moved. Built and INSTALLED per Lesson 37 (DMG sha256 `561f9bda…`, health 200 in 6 s, 157 quotes / 3063 materials / `integrity_check ok`, licence intact, 0 errors and 0 CSP violations): the Legend and Help strings 0 → 1, `tool_cost_effective` in the shell 3 → 4, and the normalised hashes — once the normaliser was fixed, see Lesson 43 — moved on exactly `index`, `CalcLegend` and `HelpTab` while `Settings`, `QuoteHistory`, `Summarize` and `LibDDL` held. S0069's die has been in its persisted price since the bulk recalculation later that day (S-DRIFT-GUARD).
>
> **Sprint S-SAVED-DRIFT — the engine moved and every file kept the old number, because Save was greyed out, 2026-09-25 (closing SHA: `66de712` via PR #429).** Opening a quote recomputes it live against its frozen `pricing_snapshot`; every xlsx, every CSV, Quote History and the Cost Breakdown list read the persisted `quote.result`. #427 and #428 changed what the engine computes for roughly 150 saved quotes, so the two parted — and nothing said so, because Save was `disabled={!isDirty || saving}` and nobody had edited anything. **Found on hardware, by the server log rather than by a test**: Henry opened `RFQ-2026-S0073` after the #428 install, saw the new Indigo setup 0.00276, pressed Save and reported it done; the log held **zero** `PATCH /quotes` after the install, and the database still read 0.0001344. The instruction he followed was mine, and I had given it three times in two days — S0059 after #426, S0069 after #427, S0073 after #428 — without once checking that the button it names was enabled (Lesson 53). S0073 and S0072 were then saved by editing a field just to light the button; S0072's edit, an IPQC tool life of 0 → 20,000, is still in the data and is harmless because IPQC carries no tool. Henry's ruling: _"hiện cảnh báo và bật nút Save"_. **What it does**: a saved quote nobody has edited is compared, field by field over `PERSISTED_RESULT_FIELDS` (exported from `calcEngine`, so the list the save writes and the list compared are one definition) at a relative tolerance of 1e-9, against what the engine computes for it now; if they differ, an amber banner shows the subtotal either side and Save lights up. Cmd+S and the button share **one** condition, `isDirty || savedDrift`; the banner hides the moment anything is edited, because it would then be describing the operator's own change. **Two decisions that are judgement, not mechanics.** (1) It compares against the **display** result, not against what Save would write: Save re-freezes the snapshot from today's library, so that comparison would flag every quote whose library prices moved and invite re-pricing old quotes wholesale — the thing the snapshot exists to prevent. This flags only what the operator can already see differ. (2) **`MARK_SAVED` writes the new snapshot back into the screen** together with the persisted result, and does not set `isDirty`. Nothing did that before — the display kept computing with the old snapshot while the database held the new one, a pre-existing mismatch that a banner turns into a loop: a quote whose library prices had moved would re-warn the instant it was saved, and no number of saves would clear it. **Validated on live data before any UI was written**: S0072 and S0073, saved after #428, stay quiet; S0069 warns on exactly the ink-setup chain, subtotal +0.0000314, which is the #427 delta; of the **78** Standard quotes carrying a snapshot, **70** warn and **8** stay quiet, and all 8 were checked to be genuinely unaffected (no priced ink row, or `setup_lm = 1`, where the old and new formulas agree) rather than missed. Quotes with no snapshot compute against the live rate table, which the offline script does not load, so they were not counted rather than guessed. **Twins**: Standard and Complex render one shared `SavedResultDriftBanner` that owns its stylesheet (#309's lesson), each calculator reads and writes its own slot, and a source guard pins all of it — sabotage-checked three ways: pointing Complex at the Standard slot (**MES-3-FIX-53's exact shape**) reddens exactly one assertion, dropping `MARK_SAVED` from one save path exactly one, and `MARK_SAVED` setting `isDirty` reddens the reducer test. **One existing guard was updated, not loosened**: `usdRateSeed.lint` pinned the load call ENDING right after the seed; the saved result now follows it, so the pattern accepts `[,)]` there and still pins the seed — swapping the seed for `0` still reddens it. Prettier had split the longer call across lines and broken both guards; the call passes `q.result` bare instead, since the reducer normalises a missing result to `null`. **Verify**: client **2261 → 2293 pass**; ESLint 0 errors / 353 warnings, identical to `main`; build green; CI 10/10. Built and INSTALLED per Lesson 37 (DMG sha256 `6c6c07f4…`, health 200, 157 quotes / 3063 materials / `integrity_check ok`, licence intact, 0 errors and 0 CSP violations): `srd-banner` 0 → 2, `pricing.drift.title` 0 → 2, the Vietnamese banner title 0 → 1 and `MARK_SAVED` 0 → 3, with `snapshot-copy-banner` holding at 3 as the control; the normalised `StandardCalc` and `ComplexCalc` hashes matched the build and `HelpTab` did not move. **Confirmed on hardware by measurement, the same day.** Henry opened S0069 — the quote the banner was built for, last saved at 04:36Z, before #427 — and pressed Save without editing anything: `PATCH /quotes/217 → 200` at 07:10:31Z, ink setup persisted as **0.0002831**, equal to the engine and to his Excel, and the subtotal as **0.020638** — exactly the number the banner had shown. Reopened, it is quiet. That no edit lit the button is measured rather than assumed: against the 24/09 backup the inputs differ only by `setup_lm` 40 → 20 and `min_gap_md` 0 → 3, both from the 04:36 save and both already inside the 0.020638 predicted before he pressed anything; the remark's `226,244 → 223,028 pcs` is derived from pitch 208 → 211; and between login and the `PATCH` the log holds only reads. **It also reopens a door #426 left shut**: `RFQ-2026-S0059` and `S0057`, the two files Jet exported with a 0 tool cost, now show the banner (subtotal 0.130906 → 0.132082 and 0.088733 → 0.089909), so they can be re-saved without an edit — which would also re-price them with today's engine, so whether to is Henry's call.

> **Sprint S-INK-MAKEREADY — both ink branches charged one metre of make-ready, and the source workbook said so all along, 2026-09-25 (closing SHA: `74b9ac5` via PR #428, with `c5dda39` via PR #427).** Henry asked for the ink setup and run on `MEZ64589026` (`RFQ-2026-S0069`) to be checked against his Excel, then settled the question himself before a line was written: he changed `setup_lm` **40 → 20**, and material setup halved (0.000719585 → 0.000359793) while ink setup **did not move** (0.000251653). One input, two readers, only one responding (Lesson 52). **#427 `c5dda39` — the coverage branch.** `calcInk`'s setup term is `area × width_m × <length> / coverage`, which is kilograms only when `<length>` is in metres. It read the material's `usage` through `ink.base_mat`, wrong twice over: `base_mat` has held a **width** since MES-3-FIX-40 renamed that column — measured, **258** ink rows carry one and **zero** match a material code (`'340'`, `'270'`, `'255'`…), so the lookup never resolved and fell back to 1 — and even resolved, `usage` is a per-piece multiplier, while the make-ready length is `setup_lm`, the field `calcMat` already charges. So material setup billed `setup_lm` metres and ink setup one metre of the same make-ready. It now resolves `setup_lm` from the named material, else the first visible Main.Mat; Henry's sheet gives **0.000283** for his row and the engine now returns 0.0002830556. **#428 `74b9ac5` — the Indigo branch, and the source that settles both.** The team's workbook, `2. TEMPLATES/Costing/CCL vina (Samsung).xlsm`, cell **T33**: `IF(C33="Indigo", cc × clicks × ROUNDUP(VLOOKUP(D33,B11:G20,6)/0.98, 0), price × (setup_kg + area × width × VLOOKUP(D33,B11:G20,6)/coverage)) / MOQ`. The materials block is headed **F = Usage, G = Setup lm**, and column 6 of B:G is **G** — in both branches. The port mapped index 6 to `usage`, the fifth column, in both; #427 had fixed one. Through the dead lookup `usage` was always 1, so Indigo setup was `⌈1 / 0.98⌉` = **2 frames on every row** — it matched the app's stored value on 19 of 21 rows, the other two being legacy quotes with no snapshot. Henry's sheet for `RFQ-2026-S0073`: `0.0084 × 8 × 41 / 1000` = **0.002755**; the app said 0.0001344 and now says 0.0027552. The `setup_lm` resolution moved above the branch so both read one length (Lesson 48). **How the decision was actually made, recorded because I nearly steered it wrong**: I first recommended keeping 2 frames as a named constant — a digital press burns no clicks threading paper — and retracted my own correct reading that `0.98` is the 980 mm frame, because the Legend called it a "setup-sheet yield". The workbook showed the Legend was describing the port, and the first reading was right. Henry chose the workbook: _"sửa nhánh Indigo đọc setup_lm đi"_. Whether the make-ready metres on an Indigo job were printed or only threaded is answered by following Excel, not proven. **A float trap found by sweeping, not sampling** (Lesson 47): `19.6 / 0.98` is `20.000000000000004`, so a bare `ceil` bills 21 frames for exactly 20. Eight hand-picked values showed nothing; a sweep of `setup_lm` from 0.05 m to 2000 m found **73**. The guard is the epsilon `calcPitch` already uses, and removing it reddens exactly the test that pins it; a `> 0` branch keeps a missing material at `0` rather than `Math.ceil(-1e-9)`, which is `-0` and fails `assert.strictEqual` (`Object.is`). **Deliberate breaks two and three of the 20-year contract**, both through `loadFrozenQuote`'s playbook: fixtures regenerated, the pre-change copies kept under `__fixtures__/pre-ink-makeready/`, and the migration test allowlisting `INK_SETUP_CHAIN` — which caught `s_mat_cost`/`g_mat_cost` moving, because MES-3-FIX-47 established that `s_mat_cost` aggregates ink despite its name. Dropping the allowlist reddens exactly 6 of 8 fixtures. **The controls invert between the two PRs, which is the evidence for both**: #427 moved the three coverage fixtures and left Indigo byte-identical; #428 moved Indigo (`bd_ink_setup` 0.6 → 15.6, 2 → 52 frames) and left the three byte-identical — and needed no third archive, since its pre-change Indigo fixture is byte-identical to the copy #427 kept. **Blast radius, the fixed engine over live data**: #427 — **130 of 153** quotes recompute higher, median +$0.000345/pc, the largest `RFQ-2026-S0017` 0.067603 → 0.471909 (11.55% of its selling price), checked rather than assumed: MOQ 100, silkscreen at $570/kg, 50 m × 0.25 m × 10% = 1.25 m² ÷ 30 m²/kg = $23.75 over 100 pieces = $0.2375/pc, of which the app charged a fiftieth. #428 — **all 26** quotes with an Indigo row, median +$0.0029/pc, S0072 and S0073 +5.28% of selling price. **A commercial consequence, reported rather than acted on**: re-saved after #428, S0072 and S0073 carry subtotals of 0.050489 and 0.050519 against a $0.0496 selling price — below cost by the team's own workbook. **Surveyed and reported, not fixed** (SS, LP, Flexo and the Indigo consumables all share the coverage branch #427 fixed): the VN coverage key `SS(Glue)` lacks the space the dropdown sends (`SS (Glue)`), so its auto value never shows and all **10** live glue rows hand-type 30 — but the number is the real question, since the six other sites say 300 and VN's own library 20, a **15×** spread no code can resolve, and fixing the space first would silently auto-fill 20; `Indigo(Oil)` with a capital O on two legacy rows (quotes 43, 60), the `===` family again; a 1-click charge of 0.030036 against 0.0074 for two or more, so one colour costs twice two, used by no live row; and S0069's pitch, which Excel takes as 211 = 208 + a 3 mm gap the app's `min_gap_md` left blank until Henry filled it — whether it should default to 3 is open. **Two errors of mine.** My first blast-radius pass reported ink setup at **$1.29/pc** and I nearly stopped the work over it — the script printed kilograms and I read dollars; quote 107 is MOQ 250,000 with a real `setup_lm` of 1230. And S0073 was re-saved between two of my reads (`min_gap_md` 0 → 3), so the second disagreed with the first and was one sentence from being reported as "two pitches on one quote"; re-reading the whole state in one query settled it. **When the operator is editing while you measure, read everything you compare in one pass.** **Verify**: client **2242 → 2256 → 2261 pass**, server 1238 / 1 chronic skip; tests written first and watched failing with the app's own wrong values (`0.00025165277777777783`, then `0.0001344`, i.e. 2 frames); ESLint 0 errors / 353 warnings, identical to `main`; CI 10/10 on both. The Legend was updated in both languages both times; two sentences that record an EARLIER refresh keep their formula and gain a dated note, because rewriting them would misstate what that refresh did. Built and INSTALLED per Lesson 37, 157 quotes / 3063 materials / `integrity_check ok` and 0 errors / 0 CSP both times: #427 DMG `1b079fea…` (two added strings 0 → 1, the old formula 1 → 0, the Indigo line holding at 1 as the control; engine chunk `5acd…` → `591e…`, matching the build), #428 DMG `382607f5…` (`setup-sheet yield` and `⌈base_usage / 0.98⌉` 1 → 0, two added strings 0 → 1, a historical sentence holding at 1; engine `591e…` → `e5f1…`); `HelpTab` unchanged both times. **On hardware**: Henry confirmed S0073 on screen (_"tôi check ok rồi"_) — but the save that followed never reached the server, which is S-SAVED-DRIFT's story. S0073 and S0072 persisted at 06:37:57Z and 06:40:59Z after an edit lit the button, and S0069 at 07:10:31Z through the banner, with ink setup 0.0002831.

> **Sprint S-TOOL-COST-READERS — four readers of a tool cost that lives on another tab, and a fifth disagreement found while writing it down, 2026-09-24 (closing SHA: `612233b` via PR #426, with `1c801c1` via PR #425).** A process can take its tool cost from the **Layout** tab (`$25.21 Plate` and `$234.24 Cutter 1` on Henry's `MEZ64589026`); it then carries a `tool_cost_src` and leaves its own `tool_cost` at **0**, and the money path resolves it through `effectiveToolCost`. Measured read-only: **48** processes carry a `tool_cost_src` and **all 48** hold `tool_cost: 0`, so any reader of the raw field reads zero for exactly the rows that have a tool (Lesson 21's shape). **#425 `1c801c1` — three items Henry reported together.** (1) The Lead time & Notice Tooling cost read `—`: `sumToolingCostStd`/`Cpx` summed the raw field, and so did the Cost Breakdown `Tooling Cost (USD)` column that exports to CSV — a column silently **wrong** rather than absent, the S-EXPORT-SETUP-GAP shape. All three now resolve through `effectiveToolCost`, with the Layout map derived in one helper (`layoutCostsFor`). This was the very trap S-TOOLLIFE-UNIT recorded catching me by hand: written down as a hazard for humans while the product's own roll-up carried it. Five tests reproduce the screenshot's **259.45**, one pinning the buggy result deliberately so a revert is visibly a regression. (2) **Enter in the Remark box did nothing, and the key Henry asked for was already taken**: `useGridKeyboardNav` binds on the capture phase, maps Enter → move down and Shift+Enter → move up, and listed `TEXTAREA` among the controls it drives — all five textareas on that tab. A textarea now owns Enter; arrows deliberately still step rows. Source-level guard, sabotage-checked: removing the exemption reddens exactly its two assertions. (3) **Move a process row ▲▼**, Std and Cpx together (Lesson 48). It swaps with the next **visible** neighbour, because these grids hide rows rather than delete them, and a swap with an invisible one would change the array and not the screen (Lesson 41's shape); `moveRowAmongVisible` returns the same array reference when the move is impossible, so a clamped move is not a spurious `isDirty`, and `canMoveRow` IS that comparison — the disabled state and the action share one condition (S-PIN-ACTIONABLE). **#426 `612233b` — the fourth reader, which #425's sweep missed because it lives in the server tree.** `05-processes.js` printed `proc.tool_cost`, so the xlsx TOOL COST column read 0 for every Layout-assigned row. Found by the audit Henry asked for (_"rà xem báo giá nào đã xuất file với số 0"_), not by a test: of the **15** audited `QUOTE_EXPORT` events against the **23** affected quotes, **two files went out wrong** — `RFQ-2026-S0059` and `RFQ-2026-S0057`, both exported by Jet on 2026-09-16, internal variant, TOOL COST `0.00` where it should read **$134.15** (plate 67.79 + cutter 66.36). **The price was never wrong**: backing the tool price out of the app's own persisted `result` returns 67.79 and 66.36 to the cent. The Cost Breakdown CSV is saved client-side with no server record, so which quotes were CSV-exported cannot be known. The fix persists `tool_cost_effective` into `result.rows.processes[]` and the sheet reads it back — MVP-1.5's pattern — because importing `layoutToolCost.js` server-side would break the no-server-calc lock and let the number drift from the library the client used; `!= null` keeps a genuine 0 from falling through to a stale cell. Its test failed first for my reason, not the product's (row 5 copied from the Materials section; `section()` is relative and Processes data starts at row 4). **Verify**: client **2223 → 2242**, server 1238 / 1 chronic skip; ESLint 0 errors / 353 warnings, identical to `main`; CI 10/10 on both. Built and INSTALLED per Lesson 37: #425 DMG `bb400d12…` (153 quotes / 3063 materials / `integrity_check ok`, 0 errors / 0 CSP) with `sc-row-move-btn` 0 → 3, `cgrid.proc.move_up` 0 → 3 and `Chuyển lên` 0 → 1, and the two changes that add no string checked by normalised chunk hash — `StandardCalc`, `ComplexCalc` and `Summarize` moved, `HelpTab` did not; #426 DMG `94309f0f…` (155 quotes) with `tool_cost_effective` 0 → 2 in the INSTALLED `05-processes.js`, the only marker that speaks for the server half, and `sc-row-move-btn` holding at 3 as the control. One marker was rejected on the way: `MOVE_PROCESS_ROW` read **2** on the running bundle before anything was installed, because `REMOVE_PROCESS_ROW` contains it (Lesson 43). **Verification, stated rather than implied**: Henry confirmed neither on screen — he moved on to the ink question. The #426 install report asked him to open S0059, press Save and export again, and that step **could not work**: Save was greyed out on an unedited quote, the gap S-SAVED-DRIFT closed a day later. S0059 is still last saved 2026-09-16, and both of Jet's files still say 0. What IS measured, on 2026-09-25: every quote saved since #426 carries the effective cost on its Layout-assigned rows — `RFQ-2026-S0075` (64.09, 345.60), `S0076` (440, 50, 130), `S0069` (25.21) — **and S0069's other row is where this entry stops being a closed story.** **Found while writing this entry, reported and deliberately not fixed**: S0069's cutter row persisted **0** while Layout computes **234.24** from the same state and library. That row (P6) is a `Die_Cut` holding the Magnetic Rotary die with **no workcenter**: the cut runs inline on P2's Brotech press, P2 already carries the plate, and `tool_cost_src` takes one source per row, so a second row is the only way to carry the die. `calcProcess` returns all zeros for a row without a workcenter — tooling included — before it ever reaches `effectiveToolCost`; the roll-up has no such gate. So #425's sentence that the roll-up and the money path _"cannot disagree about what a tool costs"_ is true about the cost and false about the charge: Lead time and the CSV column say **$259.45**, while the price carries the plate alone (tooling 0.000157545/pc). The die is **+0.001464/pc** — 234.24 ÷ (0.8 × 200,000), measured with the engine and matching that arithmetic — which is **5.3%** of S0069's $0.0276 selling price. One live quote of 106 Standard has this shape; no Complex sub-product does and nothing in Trash does. Two readings, and they are Henry's call rather than mine: a tool-only row is a legitimate way to carry a die, and `calcProcess` should charge its tooling before the workcenter gate; or it is an incomplete row, and the roll-up should drop it while the grid says why. See Lesson 41. **Ruled 2026-09-25 for the first reading** — a tool-only row is legitimate on an in-line print+cut press — and shipped as `2230d50` (PR #431, Sprint S-TOOL-ONLY-ROW). The paragraph above is left as written because it is what was known when it was written.
>
> **Sprint S-COPY-RATE-RACE — the copy path shipped inert, and three checks each said it was fine, 2026-09-22 (closing SHA: `5b2cfd4` via PR #421).** Henry, testing the day's own work: copying `RFQ-2026-S0003` still showed **26,341** while New correctly showed 26,090. **Nothing was red, and the first three things measured all came back clean** — the reducer, driven directly with his numbers, returns **26090** for copy + seed 26090 over source 26341; the endpoint was genuinely called, with **17 hits** in the server log in the `/quotes` → ~100 ms → `/latest-usd-rate` pattern that IS the copy path; and the source reads exactly as intended. **What was wrong is WHEN the answer arrived.** The rate lookup was nested inside the quote-list `.then()`, and `clearPendingQuote()` ran synchronously right after launching it — that clear changes the effect's dependency, React tears the effect down, its cleanup sets `cancelled = true`, and the nested promise resolving a **measured ~50 ms** later hit `if (cancelled) return;` and never called `loadQuote` with the seed. The unseeded fallback in the sibling `.catch` is what the operator saw. **This is Lesson 45 one layer down** — correct code that does not run — in promise ordering rather than component mounting, and the teardown was triggered by the same handler that was waiting on the promise. **The fix removes the window rather than widening it**: both fetches start together in `fetchPendingQuote` and resolve together, leaving ONE cancellation check and a synchronous block in which to seed, load and clear. The rate is fetched **only** for a copy — not asking is stronger than asking and discarding, because a request never made cannot be applied by a later edit that forgets which branch it is in — and a failed lookup yields `seed: null` so the quote still opens. **The guard's two structural assertions were rewritten, not deleted**, because the structure they described genuinely changed: whether opening re-rates a quote now lives in `pendingQuoteLoad.js` and is covered by a REAL executable test rather than by grepping for a branch, which is a strictly better home for it. One of them first failed for my own reason — it counted every `loadQuote` call including the restore-a-version and resolve-a-conflict sites, which legitimately pass no `action` — and was scoped rather than loosened. **A red herring worth recording**: seven quotes share the number `RFQ-2026-S0003`, at 26,323 and 26,341, so the rate Henry saw on open (#107) and the one he saw on copy were different rows, not a second bug. **Verify**: client **2217 → 2223 pass**; ESLint 0 errors / 353 warnings; prettier clean; build green; CI 10/10. Built and INSTALLED per Lesson 37 (DMG sha256 `d671a8a9…`, health 200, 150 quotes / 3063 materials / `integrity_check ok`, licence intact, 0 errors and 0 CSP violations). **The markers are the Lesson-43 case in miniature**: `fetchPendingQuote` and `pendingQuoteLoad` measured **0 on the installed bundle AND 0 on the fresh build** — minified away, proving nothing — and were rejected rather than reported as a failed install. What was used instead: `getLatestUsdRate` **3 → 4**, plus the normalised chunk-content hash changing on `StandardCalc` and `ComplexCalc` with **`HelpTab` unchanged as the control**. **Confirmed on hardware 2026-09-24, and the Complex half is settled by MEASUREMENT rather than by report — which is the part worth keeping.** Henry: _"đã verify ok"_. The **copy** re-test is his report. The **Complex persistence** — the MES-3-FIX-53 risk S-USD-RATE-CONFIRM-EDIT left outstanding — was measured, and it discriminates in the way the first S0003 open-test did not: quote **96** is `type = complex`, and the pre-save backup `ops_20260923_190000.sqlite` (taken `19:00Z`, **six hours before** the save, `integrity_check ok`) holds it with **no `usd_rate` at all**, last saved 2026-03-30. Live it reads **26090** in `state_json` AND `raw_json` — both stores, per Lesson 34 — with `PATCH /quotes/96` answering **200** at `01:07:07.296Z` against a `saved_at` of `01:07:07.020Z`. A write mis-routed through the Standard action lands in `stdState` and is lost on save, so that row would have come back still reading none, which is exactly what the backup shows it had. **The before-value being ABSENT rather than coincidentally equal is what makes it discriminating**: quote 98 already carried 26,090 on 2026-09-21 and **four** rows share `RFQ-2026-C0001`, so a test against any of those would have proved nothing — the same red herring as the seven `RFQ-2026-S0003` rows, one screen over. Sequence: a single `latest-usd-rate` at `01:06:30Z` and the save **37 s later**; a plain Open never asks for the rate, so a New or a Copy did happen. Day clean — **0 errors, 0 CSP violations, 0 client_error**, health 200, 150 quotes, `integrity_check ok`. **Stated rather than implied, because the log cannot say it**: which quote was copied, and whether the 26090 arrived through the dialog or was typed into the RFQ card. Both write through `setCplxField`, so the routing proof holds either way — but the copy re-test against a source whose rate differs from 26,090 rests on Henry's report, not on a measurement of mine. **A slip of my own, recorded because it nearly became the S-TIER-DROPDOWN mistake a second time**: the first grep for quote writes was `(POST|PATCH) /quotes`, and the log is JSON (`"m":"POST","p":"/quotes"`), so the space made it unmatchable and it returned **0**. I was one sentence from reporting that no save had reached the server — the identical wrong conclusion, about the identical question, that this file already records me drawing in September. See Lesson 43.
>
> **Sprint S-USD-RATE-CONFIRM-EDIT — correct the rate on the dialog that asks about it, 2026-09-22 (closing SHA: `2a65ab3` via PR #418).** Henry, after seeing the confirm dialog work on hardware: if the rate has changed he should be able to fix it there. **That is the right place for it** — whoever is being asked to confirm a number is the person who knows whether it is right, and sending them back to another tab to change one field, then through the dialog again, is the friction this feature exists to remove. _“Go back and edit”_ beside a read-only number was also describing the only option there used to be; the button is now just _Go back_ and the body says to change it here. **Confirm is REFUSED on a non-positive rate rather than writing it**: a zero silently zeroes both VND mirrors (#311), and the one dialog whose purpose is to make the operator LOOK at this number must not be the thing that accepts a bad one. The field is `DecimalInput`, the same widget both calculators already bind `usd_rate` to — a hand-rolled input would parse differently from the field it writes into, which nobody finds until a rate with a separator lands wrong. Complex writes through `setCplxField` and Standard through `setStdField`, guarded in **both** directions (**MES-3-FIX-53**: routing Complex through the Standard action lands the write in `stdState` and loses it on save — 36 days of silent data loss in May). `.hg-rate` was removed as an orphan this change created: the paragraph it styled became the input. **The CI lint incident is the part worth keeping.** The build went red at **414 warnings against a cap of 413, zero errors** — one warning, mine. The dialog reset its input with a `useEffect` syncing state to a prop, which `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` flags; that rule lives in the ROOT `eslint-plugin-react-hooks` 7.1.1 and not in `client/`'s 7.0.1, and the lint job runs `npm ci` at the root only, so it fires on CI and is invisible locally — the #295 finding, collected in person. Fixed by removing the effect entirely rather than raising the cap: the calculators pass a `key` tied to the inherited rate, React remounts, `useState` re-initialises. No effect, no second render, and a better pattern than the one it replaced. **A failed measurement of mine, recorded because the next person will try the same thing**: attempting to reproduce CI's count locally by hiding `client/node_modules` reported **22,964 warnings and 22,255 errors** — it breaks eslint's config resolution rather than simulating a root-only install, so it measured nothing at all. CI's number is the only one that settles this, and it came back at exactly **413**, which is what proves the change added precisely one and now adds none. **Verify**: client **2211 → 2217 pass**; ESLint 0 errors, 413 on CI = the `main` baseline; prettier clean; CI 10/10. Built and INSTALLED per Lesson 37 (DMG sha256 `06f01188…`, health 200, 150 quotes / 3063 materials / `integrity_check ok`, licence intact, 0 errors and 0 CSP violations). **Markers spanned both directions** per Lesson 43 — `hg-rate-inp` 0 → 1 and `rate_notice.back` 0 → 2 for what arrived, `rate_notice.edit` **2 → 0** and `Go back and edit` **1 → 0** for what left. **Hardware, confirmed by Henry on the parent change**: New seeds 26,090 and the dialog renders with its provenance (`RFQ-2026-S0068 · 22/09/2026`), and opening an old quote keeps its own rate. **That second one needed a DISCRIMINATING quote and got one**: 28 of the 91 rated quotes already carry 26,090, so opening one of those passes trivially and proves nothing. Henry opened `RFQ-2026-S0003`, whose saved rate is **26,323**, and it read 26,323 with no dialog — which is what actually establishes that opening a quote does not re-rate it, and therefore that a quote already sent to a customer cannot silently re-price itself when someone opens it to look. Still outstanding: Copy, Complex, and the editable dialog itself (correcting the rate there, and Confirm refusing a zero). **All three closed 2026-09-24, and the sentence is left as written because what closed each one differs.** Copy needed a FIX before it could be checked at all — the seed lost a race and every copy silently kept the source rate (`5b2cfd4`, PR #421). Complex and the editable dialog needed only a LOOK, and got one. The measurement that settles the MES-3-FIX-53 half is recorded at the end of Sprint S-COPY-RATE-RACE above. Reading "still outstanding" here and stopping is the failure this pointer exists to prevent — a deferral nobody re-checks decays silently (Lesson 49), and an item that was quietly finished reads exactly like one that was quietly dropped.
>
> **Sprint S-USD-RATE-INHERIT — the rate a new quote starts from, and the one thing said about it, 2026-09-22 (closing SHA: `4819607` via PR #416).** Henry: a new or copied RFQ should take the USD rate from the most recently saved quote — _"nếu tôi cần update tôi sẽ tự điền"_ — instead of being prompted and **blocked** from leaving the tab with a blank one. **Two measurements taken before designing changed the design.** The rate barely moves — **27 of the last 30** live quotes carry 26,090 — so inheriting is sound. But **59 of the 149** carry no usable rate at all, so _"most recently saved"_ and _"most recent that HAS a rate"_ are different questions, and taking the newest unconditionally would seed a zero about **40% of the time**: the blank field this exists to remove. **The navigation gate is deliberately UNTOUCHED, and that is a widening of the instruction, stated rather than buried.** `usd_rate` stays in `HEADER_GATE_FIELDS`; after seeding it is blank only when there was nothing to inherit — a fresh install, or every prior quote saved without a rate — which is exactly the case #311 wrote it for, since a blank rate zeroes both VND mirrors in silence. So the block still covers what it protects and the operator stops meeting it on an ordinary quote, which was the complaint. One line, and Henry can remove it. **What is new is the acknowledgement**: a rate nobody set is the hazard #345 recorded on the Target price — the operator meets a number already in the box and cannot tell it from one they typed — so on the way out of the RFQ tab it is stated WITH its provenance, **once**, and retired the moment they edit it, because confirming a number you just typed is the alarm people learn to click through. **Open is untouched and that is the load-bearing line**: a saved quote keeps the rate it was quoted at, or a quote already sent to a customer would silently re-price itself when somebody opened it to look. **Copy DOES take the current rate**, since a copy is a new RFQ and the source's may be months old — #345's rule applied to the rate. **Correction, same day**: the Copy half of that sentence did NOT work when it shipped — the seed lost a race and copies silently kept the source's rate. Left as written because the rule it states is still the design; `5b2cfd4` (PR #421, Sprint S-COPY-RATE-RACE above) is where it became true. Rewriting it to read correctly would hide that the first cut shipped inert, which is the part worth keeping. **The acknowledgement is component state, never quote state**: persisting it would ride into every save and into the signed `_Schema` payload of every export for something only one screen reads, which is what `target_contr` did for months. **Its own server route** (`GET /shared/latest-usd-rate`) rather than a field derived from the quote list, because that list returns the full quote objects and is **26.6 MB to read one number**. **Verify**: server **1156 pass** / 1 chronic skip, client **2194 → 2211**; ESLint 0 errors / 353 warnings, same as `main`; prettier clean; build green; CI 10/10. Built and INSTALLED per Lesson 37 (DMG sha256 `af69db31…`, health 200, 150 quotes / 3063 materials / `integrity_check ok`, licence intact, 0 errors and 0 CSP violations, embedded server ready in 897 ms). **Five markers across BOTH halves** — `rate_notice.title` 0 → 2, `rate_notice.confirm` 0 → 2, `latest-usd-rate` 0 → 1 and `hg-rate` 0 → 1 in the client bundle, and `latest-usd-rate` **0 → 2 in the installed `server/routes/shared.js`**, which is the only one that speaks for the server half. **A live check was nearly trusted and is recorded in Lesson 43**: calling the new route anonymously answers 401 — and so does the build without it, and so does a path invented on the spot, because the auth middleware runs before routing. **The sabotage checks repeated the previous PR's exact mistake**: `<UsdRateNoticeModalX>` still contains `<UsdRateNoticeModal>`, so the twin-parity assertion passed a forked calculator until the trailing character class was added — the same loose-substring error made one PR earlier, by the person who had just written it down. **Not verified by this session**: the route's first AUTHENTICATED call, which needs Henry's password.
>
> **Sprint S-MATLIB-ROW-MENU — Open, Copy and Delete on a material row, 2026-09-22 (closing SHA: `52473e3` via PR #415).** Open is what a left-click already did; **Copy and Delete had no affordance on the row at all** — delete lived inside the edit modal, and duplicating a material meant retyping it, which the live library shows people doing: runs of near-identical rows differing only in a thickness or a PE side. **ONE menu component for all three tabs, not three** — the tabs are already near-duplicates (MES-3-FIX-56) and a per-tab menu is three places for these actions to drift apart. Placement, edge-flipping and drag come from the shared `useFloatingMenu`, the hook Quote History's menu already uses, so a right-click on the last visible row is not clipped by the table's own overflow; the CSS mirrors that menu but **owns its own classes**, because borrowing `.qh-ctx-*` works only while both tabs land in one chunk and loses its styling silently the moment they do not (#309). **Copy opens the Add modal PRE-FILLED and saves nothing**, and that is the invariant S-MATLIB-SAVE-ONCE created the risk around a day earlier: `handleAdd` now persists, so a Copy routed through it would put a second row under a near-identical name on the server before anyone had looked at it. `copySeed` suffixes the name for a related reason — names are a **join key**, and two rows normalising to one make `resolveLibRow` refuse both and blank the library columns on somebody else's quote (Lesson 32) — and the suffix is deliberately **not translated**, because it lands inside data, so a localised one would make the same copy produce a different name depending on who was logged in. The seed takes **each tab's own** idea of a new row rather than a reconstruction (NPI stamps today and a currency, Sourcing a month, IFS neither): a copy is a new row, and inheriting the source's Update Date is what dated live rows 2029. **The guard earned itself on its first run by catching a gap in the PRODUCT, not in itself** — the view-only gate was missing from all three `onContextMenu` handlers, so a view-only operator would have been handed Copy and Delete. Cause in Lesson 51. **Two of three sabotage checks then found a weakness in the assertions**: `<RowContextMenuNPI>` still contains `<RowContextMenu>`, so the duplication check passed a forked menu until a trailing character class was added — a mistake repeated verbatim one PR later (see S-USD-RATE-INHERIT), which is why it is written down rather than quietly fixed. **Verify**: client **2182 → 2194 pass**; ESLint 0 errors / 353 warnings, same as `main`; prettier clean; build green; CI 10/10.
>
> **Sprint S-MATLIB-SAVE-ONCE — the card's Save did not save, 2026-09-22 (closing SHA: `da9ee2a` via PR #413).** Adding a material took TWO clicks: **Save** on the card wrote the row into the tab's own state, and a separate **Save Changes** chip in the tab bar did the POST. Nothing on the card said so, so a row could be typed, saved, and lost by switching tabs. Edit and delete go the same way — leaving delete on the old flow would make the chip appear **only** after a delete, which is harder to explain than either rule on its own. **The one way to get this wrong fails silently, and that is what the change is really about.** `setData` does not flush before the handler returns, so a save that reads this component's state posts the arrays as they were **before** the edit: the request succeeds, the chip clears, the screen still shows the new row **from local state** — and the row is not on the server. It comes back missing on the next load with nothing having reported an error. So each handler builds `next`, hands **that same array** to `saveNow`, and `persist` spreads the patch **after** its own state so the caller wins. `materialLibrarySave.lint.test.js` pins both halves, sabotage-checked in both directions: reversing the spread reddens exactly one test, pointing one handler at `data` reddens exactly one other. **The guard went red on its first run for its OWN reason**, which is the recurring trap rather than a new one: `persist(patch = {})` carries `{}` in a DEFAULT PARAMETER, so brace-matching from the first `{` after the function name captured that and returned an empty body — reported as _“persist must catch”_, indistinguishable from the product having lost its try/catch. Fixed by matching PARENS first. **The chip is kept and its meaning changes**: `persist` raises it in the `catch`, so when it appears now it means _the last save failed_ — at that moment the edit exists only in local state and the chip is the only way back without re-typing. **Cost measured rather than assumed, because it moved from per-batch to per-action**: a save posts all three libraries, **~7 MB**, which was already true of the chip. `/save-all` writes **no** snapshot, so this does not multiply the 29 MB `auto_*` backups — those come from the scheduler. Overlapping saves are safe because each payload is the full current state rather than a delta, so whichever lands last is correct either way. **Second half**: a faded clear button in all three search boxes, returning the caret to the field — clearing means "start over", and leaving focus on a button that just unmounted itself is worse than not having it. The count badge moves left of it **unconditionally**, because the badge only renders while a search is active, which is exactly when the button is there. The input's right padding is **constant** rather than applied only while the button shows, or the text shifts on the first keystroke. **Verify**: client **2174 → 2182 pass**; ESLint 0 errors / 353 warnings, the same count as `main`, with **no warning on any touched file**; prettier clean; build green; CI 10/10. Built and INSTALLED per Lesson 37 (DMG sha256 `33aa7683…`, health 200, 149 quotes / 3063 materials / `integrity_check ok`, licence intact, 0 errors and 0 CSP violations in the log). **The markers spanned BOTH directions** — `ml-hb-search-clear` 0 → 1 and `matlib.search_clear` 0 → 2 for what was added, and **`markDirty` 1 → 0** for what was removed, which is the stronger half: it shows the two-step mechanism is GONE from the running build rather than merely that new code arrived beside it. `saveNow` measured 0 on the installed bundle AND 0 on the fresh build and was rejected (Lesson 43).
>
> **Sprint S-PIN-ACTIONABLE — the margin cell was right and the alarm beside it could never be answered, 2026-09-22 (closing SHA: `72c4649` via PR #411, with `06ffab8`).** Henry pinned GM at **20%** and the cell read **20.1%**, the same on VA and Contr. **The number is not a display bug — it is what the quote actually has.** Price is stored to 4 decimals (`priceSolver.js:219`), so at a cost of 0.02382 a 20.0% target wants 0.029775, which stores as **0.0298 and reads back 20.07%**, while the neighbouring price 0.0297 reads **19.80%**. No 4-decimal price yields 20.0% on that quote at all. Henry chose to keep 4 decimals rather than change what gets quoted to a customer, so the display stays. **What WAS a defect is what happened next.** `pinDrift` compares the achieved value against the pin with a fixed **0.05pp** tolerance and 0.07pp clears it, so the cell turned amber — _“0.1% above the 20.0% you pinned. Click to re-apply at $0.0298”_ — and clicking re-solved to **the same 0.0298** and left the cue exactly where it was. **An alarm with no remedy is the one people learn to ignore**, which is the opposite of what that cue exists for and the same shape as Lesson 0, one surface over. **The fix is a composition, not a new rule, and that is the whole point.** `planAutoHold` already knew the answer: it returns `null` when the solved price rounds to the price the tier already holds — which is precisely why the auto-hold correctly did nothing while the cue shouted. The cue was simply asking a DIFFERENT question. `actionablePinDrift` runs `pinDrift` and then defers to `planAutoHold`, so **the cue and the remedy share ONE condition by construction** rather than being two conditions free to disagree — which is how this existed at all. **Measured before choosing rather than after**: 21 of the 147 live quotes would show a GM other than the one typed, **every one of them a low-price job**, because a 0.0001 step is 0.33% of a 0.03 price and 0.0002% of a 5.00 one. **Tests 2168 → 2174.** One reconstructs the real case from cost 0.02382 and asserts BOTH halves — `pinDrift` alone still calls it drifted, and the cue stays silent anyway — so a future "simplification" that drops the composition cannot pass. Another pins that cue and remedy agree across four cases, since the two drifting apart IS the bug; sabotaging the condition reddens exactly those four and no others. **ESLint caught a real placement error that nothing else could**: the two new per-row consts landed in the row map that does **not** render the margin cells, so they were out of scope at every use — and **the build passed and all 2174 tests passed**, because nothing renders under `node:test`. Only `no-undef` saw it. **`06ffab8` is a slip of mine in the same file, fixed rather than catalogued**: inserting the new function put it BETWEEN `planAutoHold`'s JSDoc and `planAutoHold` itself, orphaning the 20-line block that explains why that guard compares against the tier's CURRENT price — the reasoning S-MARGIN-HOLD-FIX paid for — and leaving `planAutoHold` undocumented while the block read as if it belonged to the new function. **Verify**: ESLint 0 errors / 353 warnings, the same count as `main`; prettier clean; build green; CI 10/10. Built and INSTALLED per Lesson 37 (DMG sha256 `ddc26b2d…`, health 200, 148 quotes / 3063 materials / `integrity_check ok`, licence still `CCL Design Vietnam- Hai Phong`, 0 errors and 0 CSP violations in the log). **The marker needed a new technique and it is written into Lesson 43**: all four identifier candidates read **0 on the installed bundle AND 0 on the fresh build** (minified away), and the chunk NAME is not usable either because this repo's build is **not deterministic** — three builds from the same clean commit produced three different `StandardCalc` hashes. Normalising the import URLs out of each chunk and hashing what remains discriminates cleanly: `StandardCalc` and `ComplexCalc` both changed, `HelpTab` was byte-identical as the control, and after the install both matched the DMG.
>
> **Sprint S-EXPORT-SAVE-PATH — the export had nowhere to go but Downloads, and on the thin CLIENT possibly nowhere at all, 2026-09-22 (closing SHA: `f114155` via PR #409).** `Export…` from Cost Breakdown or Quote History dropped the xlsx/zip straight into the browser's download folder with no say in it — a bare `<a download>` was the **only** path it had. **The missing picker is the smaller half.** The thin CLIENT loads `http://<remote-ip>:3100`, which is not a secure context, and that same anchor had already been diagnosed there once: the report was _“CSV export did nothing on CLIENT”_, and it is why `saveCsv` grew a three-tier cascade in the first place. The quote export never did, so the one topology where the anchor is least reliable had **nothing to fall back to**. `saveBlob` runs the same three tiers for binary — Electron bridge → File System Access → download anchor. **The bridge comes FIRST, and the order is the load-bearing part**: it is the only tier present on both the embedded SERVER and the insecure CLIENT, so reversing it makes the two topologies diverge while every test stays green. No new main-process surface was needed and the path whitelist was not relaxed — `ops:fs.writeFile` already accepts a `Uint8Array` and already refuses any path its own dialog did not just return. **Cancel writes nothing, returns null, and never falls through to the download folder**: a user who cancels a Save dialog and then finds the file in Downloads has been ignored, not helped. It also announces nothing — `exportQuote` now awaits the save and reports `savedTo`, and the modal suppresses `onSuccess` when it is null, because `onSuccess` pops an alert naming the file and telling someone who just pressed Cancel that their file was exported states the opposite of what happened. **`saveCsv` was deliberately NOT folded in**, and that decision is written into Lesson 48 rather than left as a silent duplicate: merging would have changed what the bridge receives on a tested, operator-facing path for no behaviour gain, so both survive and a guard pins the one property that can drift — the tier order — in both files. **Tests**: 9 on `saveBlob` covering each tier, both cancel paths, the binary bytes arriving as a `Uint8Array` rather than a string (a string would corrupt an xlsx), and the bridge winning when both APIs exist; 7 source-level. Removing the cancel gate reddens exactly that one assertion. **A Lesson-43 near-miss, and it is the same case that lesson already records rather than a new one**: the obvious marker for this change was `saveBlob`, a brand-new exported function name — and it measured **0 on the installed bundle AND 0 on the fresh build**, because rolldown minifies the name away entirely. A 0/0 pair proves nothing in either direction; had it been trusted, a correct install would have been reported as a failure and both of us would have gone looking for a bug that was not there. The markers actually used were `savedTo` (0 → 1), `showSaveDialog` (3 → 4) and — for the same day's #407 — `bd_overhead` **scoped to the Summarize chunk** (0 → 1), because unscoped it reads 1 on the installed bundle already, calcEngine having always carried the field. **Verify**: client **2152 → 2168 pass**; ESLint 0 errors / 353 warnings, the same count as `main`; prettier clean; build green; CI 10/10.
